## 2026-10-15 — T-023 worker (Haiku 5.5, attempt 1 of 2) — fleet v2 resolver: door priority, billing and fast policy, unavailable stays unavailable

- **Did:** `resolve_v2(fleet, seat, registry, inventory)` and CLI `fleet_state.py resolve-v2 --fleet F --seat S --inventory I` (exit 1 when unavailable). Walks the seat's doors in order; skips a door whose billing class is not in `policy.billing_classes`, a `no_zdr` door when `policy.no_zdr` is `forbid`, and a door whose provider inventory status is not `success`. Takes the newest family model via the existing `choose()`, which now takes `allow_fast` (default false). The inventory name is decoded to its base before `encode_model`, so encoded inventory names work. Returns `{"source": "unavailable", "model": null}` when no door yields a model; no `FALLBACKS` or guessed id is used.
- **Validation added (`validate-fleet` on v2):** `speed: fast` under `policy.fast: false` is refused (`… speed fast requires fleet.policy.fast`); the seat `family` must belong to at least one door's provider (`… family 'X' unsupported for its doors`). The v2 validator takes an optional registry so the resolver can check fixture registries.
- **Interpretations (for the orchestrator to confirm):** (a) `resolve` still refuses v2 (`fleet v2 is not resolvable yet`) because the launchers call `resolve` and this task makes no launcher change; `resolve-v2` is the v2 entry point. (b) "refused" for a door means the walk never selects it; "refused" for a fast seat means `validate-fleet` rejects it. (c) The result's `provider` is the canonical name (`cursor`), while the inventory key stays the legacy name (`agent`), mapped through `legacy_view`.
- **Launcher behaviour with a v2 fleet (item 4, unchanged code):** `validate-worker` and `validate-brain` call `approved_fleet` → `validate_fleet`, which now accepts a valid v2 fleet (the path and hash checks are unchanged). Neither reads v2 seats. `spawn-worker.sh` and `spawn-brain.sh` then run `fleet_state.py resolve`, which refuses v2 with `ERROR: … fleet v2 is not resolvable yet`, so a v2 launch stops before any provider call. Verified by reading the scripts; not executed.
- **Fixture-only paths:** every real door is `included` and allows ZDR, so the credits, api and `no_zdr` cases use fixture registries built from the real one with extra doors. The fall-through case uses a fixture claude door, because cursor (`standard`/`fast`) and codex (`unknown`) share no speed, so a mixed cursor/codex seat cannot validate.
- **Checks (worker, exit codes):** baseline `shellcheck scripts/*.sh tests/*.sh` 0, `test-fleet-v2.py` 0, `test-fleet-state.py` 0. After the change: `test-fleet-v2.py` 0 (its only skip notice is `jsonschema` not installed), `test-fleet-state.py` 0, `validate-fleet` on the frozen `fleet.json` 0, shellcheck 0, no-`__pycache__` check 0. `bash tests/run-tests.sh` was not run by the worker (standing 2A rule); it remains an orchestrator verification command.
- **Not proven:** a mutation check (remove a billing, no_zdr, health or fast guard in a scratch copy and confirm the focused test fails) was attempted, but the sandbox denied that command, so it was not run. The negatives are untested against mutations; the orchestrator may want that proof before acceptance.
- **Unrelated working-tree change:** `docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation/GOAL.md` shows an uncommitted `****` appended to a table separator line (line 99). It is not from this worker, it is not in the allow-list, and it was left unstaged and uncommitted.
- **Result:** Scoped change committed (`scripts/fleet_state.py`, `tests/test-fleet-v2.py`, this entry). No feature acceptance and no gate or status change. F004 remains `passes: false`: the drift events, recorded resolution in the spec, the chair seat, and the launcher wiring are not addressed by T-023.

  - (e) Optional: a `jsonschema`-gated command that validates the persisted specs against `schemas/execution-spec.schema.json`. This kills M. Same pattern as T-019-R1.
  - Allow-list: `tests/test-execution-integration.py`, `tests/test-execution-spec.py`, PROGRESS.md.
- **Non-blocking notes:**
  - (a) The observation is only as trustworthy as the log file. A worker can append lines to its own `.tasks/logs/<run_id>.log`, which is gitignored and outside the scope guarantee. A forged codex `turn.completed` would add to the observed usage. Claude and Cursor take the last `result`, and their real one comes last. A forged Cursor init downgrades the model to `unknown`. Reading from a pipe (tee) would close this. That belongs to 2B anti-gaming, not 2A.
  - (b) After acceptance, re-smoke S3 should confirm a real contract-6 receipt carries observed values. The first candidate is this run's own receipt (claude JSON). I cannot see it from inside the run, so I do not claim it.

- **L-016 candidate:** a test for a property that an outer layer also provides (bash async-list `/dev/null`, a verifier's `DEVNULL`) must drive the inner layer directly, or it proves nothing about that layer.

Verdict: PASS

- **(1) Existing receipts — confirmed.** I loaded all 99 files in `.tasks/receipts/` with the old and the new `load_receipt`. The results are identical: 76 UUID receipts give `ok v1`. The 23 Phase-1 legacy `worker-*`/`brain-*`/`latest-*` files fail with the same error in both versions (`not canonical JSON`; this predates T-019). Every file's SHA-256 is the same before and after. No `observation` key is required for v1.
- **(2) Observation block in code — confirmed; schema file — FAILS (see blocking issue).** `validate_observation` requires the exact keys at all three levels. `source` must be one of `observed|estimated|unknown`. `unknown` ⇔ `null` holds both ways. Text values must be non-empty `str`. Counts must be non-bool `int` ≥ 0. With no `observation`, `build_receipt` writes `observe_output.all_unknown()`.
- **(3) No spec or seat model can become `observed` — confirmed.** The only writer is `execution_runner.py:143` `write_receipt(...)`, and it passes no `observation`. The `write` CLI has no observation argument. `observe_output.observe(provider, raw)` cannot see the spec. So every new receipt is all-`unknown` until T-020.
- **(4) Receipt guards untouched — confirmed from the diff.** `_atomic_create` (no-clobber, 0400), `capture_before`, `git_delta`, the spec-hash binding, the scope recomputation, the commit/changed-path rechecks and `receipt_link` are byte-unchanged. The changes are limited to: the import, the `FIELDS_V1`/`FIELDS` split, `validate_observation`, the `observation=None` parameter, `schema_version: 2`, and a version dispatch at the top of `validate_receipt`. `execution_runner.py`, `execute_provider.py`, `verification_record.py`, `verify-mission.sh` and both launchers have no diff. The launchers snapshot only the shell script, and Python runs from live `scripts/`, so the new `observe_output` import resolves (both the manifest and the integration fixture list it).
- **(5) Mutations (scratch copy, `tests/test-execution-receipt.py`; baseline 3 tests OK; the file was restored and `cmp` shows it identical):**
  - Caught (exit 1): A v2 skips `validate_observation`; C `unknown` with a value; E negative usage; F extra observation key; G build writes v1; K default records the spec model as `observed`.
  - **Survives:** B, the `source` enum check removed. The "bad source" case uses `source: "guessed", value: null`, so the null check rejects it first. This negative is vacuous.
  - Survives, untested: H v1 receipt carrying an `observation`; I bool as usage; J non-text model.
  - Survives, equivalent: D (the type check also rejects null).
- **(6) Verifier — confirmed.** `verification_record.py` reads named receipt fields only and never the receipt's `schema_version` or `observation`. Old receipts load as in (1). New v2 receipts go through `spawn-worker.sh` → `verify-mission.sh --run-id` in `tests/test-verification-record.py`, which was command 3 (exit 0) of the passing VerificationRecord `verification/a80bf0df….json`.
- **BLOCKING — the JSON schema rejects every receipt, v1 and v2.** `$defs.receiptBody` has `additionalProperties: false`, but its `properties` no longer list `schema_version` (they still did at the old root), and they do not list `observation`. In draft 2020-12, keys declared only in a sibling `allOf` branch count as additional. Evidence (Draft202012Validator; both schemas pass `check_schema`):
  - Old schema: 76/76 real receipts valid.
  - New schema: 0/76 valid, plus a synthetic v2 all-unknown receipt invalid. For the T-019 receipt the error is `Additional properties are not allowed ('schema_version' was unexpected)` in both branches.
  - Probe in scratch: declaring `schema_version` (`enum [1,2]`) and `observation` (`$ref observation`) in `receiptBody` makes 76/76 v1 and the v2 receipt valid. It also accepts a v1 receipt that carries `observation`, so the v1 branch must also forbid that key.
  - Effect: the manifest installs this file into consumers, and T-027 reads it. Objective 3 ("update the JSON schema, `oneOf` v1/v2") is not met. Nothing in the suite validates against the schema, so this passed unnoticed.
  - Second mismatch: the schema's `nonempty` pattern rejects text with leading/trailing whitespace, but the code accepts it. Pick one rule.
- **Proposed correction, T-019-R1 (feature F003; any economy seat; Architect review):**
  - Allow-list: `schemas/execution-receipt.schema.json`, `tests/test-execution-receipt.py`, PROGRESS.md. No code change, unless the text rule is aligned in code instead, in which case add `scripts/execution_receipt.py`.
  - Schema fix: declare `schema_version` and `observation` in `receiptBody`; forbid `observation` in the v1 branch; make text values agree with the code.
  - Tests:
    - A schema/code agreement test with no third-party dependency: `set(receiptBody.properties) == execution_receipt.FIELDS`, `receiptBody.required == FIELDS_V1`, observation/usage key sets equal `OBSERVATION_FIELDS`/`USAGE_FIELDS`.
    - Negatives: `{"source":"guessed","value":"x"}` (kills B), v1 with `observation` (H), `true` as usage (I), `7` as model (J), v2 without `observation`.
  - Verification commands: T-019's seven commands, plus one that validates every UUID receipt in `$PURRFECT_LIVE_ROOT/.tasks/receipts` against the schema **when `jsonschema` is importable**, skipping with a printed notice otherwise.
- **Non-blocking notes:** (a) a non-hashable `source` (e.g. a list) raises `TypeError`, not `ReceiptError`. That still fails closed, but `isinstance(source, str)` first would give a clean error. (b) `schema_version` `true`/`2.0` compare equal to 1/2. This predates T-019 for v1; harmless today.

- **Answer to the worker's open question: the literal `.agents/` prefix rule is safe and right; do not widen it.**
  - In this glob language (`execution_receipt.glob_regex`), a pattern that starts with the literal `.agents/` can only match paths under `.agents/`, and `..`/`.` segments are refused. The flag therefore never mounts anything the scope does not already aim at.
  - Exactly `.agents` matches only a *file* named `.agents`, never anything under it, so it authorizes no `.agents/` write. No flag is correct.

That fails closed: the task fails visibly and nothing is widened. Widening the trigger to "any pattern that could match `.agents/`" would mount all of `.agents/` for every broad-scope task. That is less safe.
  - **Rule to record (not changed here):** a Codex task that must edit `.agents/` names literal `.agents/…` paths in its allow-list. After the S2 re-smoke, the Sandbox-limits row in the dispatch-worker skill should say this instead of "route to another seat".

    - → `".agents/" in pattern`. This would mount for `docs/.agents/x`.

It does **not** see ignored paths (`.tasks/`, `.claude/settings.local.json`) or `.git/config`/hooks. A seat could reach those only by an absolute path, and its prompt does not name one. The seats inherit the caller's whole environment, as every `spawn-worker.sh` dispatch already does.

    1. Run from a plain terminal, not from inside a spawned run.
    2. Command: `bash scripts/fleet-doctor.sh --smoke --mission docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation --timeout 300 --keep --dest <new dir outside the repo>`. `--keep` preserves the clone's receipts and logs as evidence. They hold transcripts, so do not commit them; delete the dir after recording.

    4. After the run, check `ps` for leftover provider processes.
    5. Optionally leave out `cursor.*` until T-009 (plan step S2).
- **(6) Summary location and content, PASS.** The summary goes to `<live>/.tasks/smoke/<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.json`; `.tasks/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:2`), and the file is opened with exclusive create. Fields: base commit, clone path, timestamps, mission, skipped brain note, and per seat: status, reason, run id, outcome, exit code, commit, provider/model/effort/speed, clone_reset. The only transcript-derived text is the ≤300-character `reason` described in (2). No login/account output reaches it. Without `--keep`, the dest (clone, logs, `<seat>.out`) is removed.
- **Worker's `fleet-doctor.sh --help` slip:** it ran the doctor's default probe, which runs only the registry `login_check` per provider (`fleet_doctor.py:57`), not a task or model run. Whether it counts against the budget is the Front Desk's call.

  2. a fake that writes outside its allow-list, asserting `unusable`;

- **Design questions resolved (alternative rejected):**
  1. Smoke environment: a scratch `git clone` of HEAD; each seat runs `spawn-worker.sh` inside the clone, so the clone is the workspace root and the Phase 1 `--repo` guard is unchanged. Rejected: a linked worktree (its `.git` is a file, so Codex's `--add-dir <repo>/.git` would not cover the object store and Codex would fail for a reason the live tree never has); lifting `--repo` (not allowed in Phase 1). The clone's smoke fleet is a byte copy of the frozen fleet except `mission.path`, with an `approved_by` that names the source file and hash; the live snapshot is compared before and after.
  2. Smoke result has three states (`usable`, `usable-with-limit`, `unusable`), decided from receipts and Git only, so a Codex seat with the `.agents/` limit stays usable for other work. Brain seats are not smoked; the latest completed brain receipt proves them (F007 mode launches one brain run, because its audit needs it). The real smoke is an orchestrator step, never a verification command (it spends quota).
  3. Cursor fix: new provider contract 4 (plain model ID); contracts 1-3 unchanged, so v1-v3 evidence stays byte for byte. Contract 4 refuses a model name whose effort token contradicts the spec effort. Rejected: sending `grok-4.7-xhigh` under a spec that records `medium` (false evidence).
  4. Clean-worktree verifier keeps record schema v1 and exports `PURRFECT_LIVE_ROOT` for checks that need `.tasks/` state (F007's evidence). Numbered records are a separate task, so each guard change has its own review.
  5. Seat limits: only allow-list write limits are decidable by `dispatch_policy.py`; login-state limits stay a routing rule. Rejected: parsing worker-check prose.
  6. Codex `.agents/`: answered by a direct probe (P-1, with a control run) before any code; T-013 only on PASS.
  7. Carrier feature: backlog tasks T-007, T-008, T-010, T-011, T-012 are filed under F007 (dispatch needs a pending feature; F001 is accepted). They are slice evidence only; F007 is accepted only on T-038. T-009 and T-013 to T-017 are filed under F004.
  8. Request item 6 correction: the billing-class, `no_zdr` and `priority` negatives already exist (`test_registry_type_and_legacy_name_guards`). The real gaps are fields the validator never checks (effort/fast encodings, capabilities, seat specs, fallbacks); T-017 covers them. N3 is pinned by a test and a docstring, not changed.
  9. A ledger write failure never changes a run's outcome (WARN only); drift events are folded with the ledger so they reach Git ("never silently"). F006's validator is dependency-free (`jsonschema` is not installed) and refuses schema keywords it does not implement.

**Write these tasks, in this dependency order, each tagged in a table with `category` and `size` (S/M/L) and with `review` = `Architect` or `code-reviewer`:**
1. **Real seat smoke** (`fleet-doctor.sh --smoke`): for each frozen seat (see `fleet.json`: brain, cursor.premium/economy, codex.premium/economy, agy.economy, claude.premium/economy) launch one tiny real run through the real adapter: write a file under a scratch copy of `.agents/` and `docs/`, run one check, commit in the scratch copy; report pass/fail per seat; a failing seat is unusable for the mission and the reason goes to the sandbox-limit table (dispatch-worker skill and template note). External `--repo` targets are disabled in Phase 1, so decide how a scratch copy can use the real adapter without weakening a guard (for example a scratch worktree of this repository, or a scratch repository with the scripts copied in). Pick the smallest safe design; if none is safe, say so and give the alternative. Smoke runs must not touch the live tree and must not bill credits or use fast modes.
2. **Cursor model argument fix (F004 slice 1):** `scripts/execute_provider.py` must stop sending `model[effort=…,fast=…]` for Composer; use `evidence/F004-cursor-model-argument.md` (the real rejection message is verbatim there) and build a test from that message. Keep the frozen evidence (v1-v3 specs) valid byte for byte. Note which Cursor models use effort/fast in the name and which do not.
3. **Clean-worktree verifier:** checks run in a temporary git worktree at the receipt's commit, removed afterwards; a numbered new VerificationRecord is allowed per run (old records stay immutable; acceptance cites the record hash); export `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in `spawn-worker.sh` and `spawn-brain.sh`.
4. **Codex `--add-dir .agents` test:** test whether adding `--add-dir <repo>/.agents` (only when the task allow-list includes `.agents/`) lets a Codex worker write there, the same way `--add-dir .git` works. Real-run evidence goes in a file under `evidence/`.
5. **Per-seat read/write limits in the registry,** refused by `dispatch_policy.py` before launch (task allow-list or checks that hit a limit). Known limits: Codex workspace-write cannot write under `.agents/` and cannot see Claude or agy login state.
6. **Findings N1 and N3 and the missing negative tests:** N1 `spawn-worker.sh cursor … --provider agent` rejected as a mismatch because names are compared before conversion; N3 `fleet_state.py` exits at import on a registry error and adds `scripts/` to `sys.path`; add the missing type/enum negative tests in `tests/test-registry.py` (billing class, `no_zdr`, `priority`).
7. **F003, F004 (rest), F005, F006, F007 in feature order** (`features.json` is the source of the steps; F002 was removed). F007 is a real smoke through claude, codex, cursor-agent (Composer and Grok) and agy and needs a Mac session, so give its verification commands in the verifier-only form and say which parts need a human. If F004 depends on owner decision 2 (frozen-fleet amendment), it is approved (2026-10-14 Gate 0).

**Task format (every task):** the worker template, all required sections; allow-list and deny-list; JSON-argv verification commands; a "Why each check exists" section; the long-command rule in the Iteration policy; `max_attempts: 2`; frontmatter `worker:` and `invocation:` set to `any usable economy seat (the Front Desk rotates)`; if a task must avoid a seat (for example it edits `.agents/`, so not Codex while the limit stands; or it needs login state, so not Codex), say so in a `## Seat limits` section. **Standing rules to bake in:** worker checks are focused and sandbox-safe commands only; the full suite and real-machine checks are verification commands only; tests never write into the live tree (mutate a temporary copy); until task 3 lands, verification runs in the live tree, so tasks must not leave `__pycache__` behind. **Size:** cut each task so an economy worker finishes it in one session; split guard code across several tasks rather than one large one, because every guard change gets its own Architect review.

Architect (`spawn-brain.sh`, Opus): planning, and a separate review of every guard, safety-test, verification/acceptance/dispatch change or any diff I am unsure about.

Claude guard: if the Claude window nears its limit, pause Claude dispatches (Architect, Haiku) until reset. Never credits, billed usage or fast modes; a limit error never means a paid path.
- **Order:** (1) one Architect planning run writes tasks tagged with category and size: (a) "real seat smoke" (`fleet-doctor.sh --smoke`); (b) backlog: 1 clean-worktree verifier + numbered records + `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in the spawn scripts; 2 Cursor model argument fix (F004) with a test from the real rejection message in `evidence/F004-cursor-model-argument.md`; 3 Codex `--add-dir .agents` test; 4 per-seat read/write limits in the registry refused by `dispatch_policy.py` before launch; 5 findings N1/N3 and missing type/enum negative tests; (c) F003+ in feature order. (2) Smoke first, then the Cursor fix (they unlock Cursor and agy quota), then the rest in order. (3) When F004 is done, prepare the brain-seat move to agy Opus as a drift event for the owner's approval; do not apply it. (4) After the backlog, the Architect writes a short proposal to pull parallel worktree dispatch forward from 2B (proposal only).
- **Routing (data collection):** no seat preference. Rotate economy worker tasks across all seats that pass the smoke: claude.economy (Haiku), codex.economy, cursor.economy, agy.economy. Each category should reach each usable seat in turn. Exceptions: known seat limits in the sandbox-limit table, and seats that failed the smoke; record each exception. A seat's first use tonight requires a passing smoke. After every run append one line to `routing-data.jsonl`: run_id, task, category, size, seat, model, effort, attempt, outcome, failure class (none / task / seat-limit / harness / environment), duration, tokens or cost if reported. Only "task" failures count against a model.
- **Standing rules:** worker checks are focused and sandbox-safe; the full suite and real-machine checks (logins, `fleet-doctor.sh`) are verification commands only. Every task's Iteration policy has the long-command rule. Tests never write into the live tree. Until the clean-worktree verifier lands: before every `verify-mission.sh`, check for `scripts/__pycache__` and delete it if present, recording each deletion. I never commit worker code myself: preserve uncommitted worker work as a patch under `.tasks/wip/`, restore the tree, let the next attempt apply it. Worker PROGRESS narratives are not evidence (L-009). Classify every failure (task / seat-limit / harness / environment). A non-task failure does not use up an attempt; retry on another usable seat and record why. A task that fails twice for task reasons goes back to the Architect to re-split (decomposition miss). Record every seat change and task-text change here.
- **Unattended rules:** when a stop rule fires on an item, do not wait: record what failed, the evidence and a proposal here, leave the tree clean, continue with the next independent item. Stop entirely only if nothing independent is left, or something unsafe would be needed (scope bypass, protected files or the owner's shell config, credits, fast modes). Do not accept features or advance gates; when a feature is ready, prepare its acceptance command here and continue. After every run update PROGRESS.md and commit, then push `mission/2a-foundation` (that branch only; no PRs, no tags, never main). Transcripts and logs stay under `.tasks/`; commit summaries only.
- **Morning report** goes at the top of this file when I stop or at 07:00: runs used and how many ended task-done; each failure with its class; a category x seat table of done/failed (task failures only); quota used per vendor and pause durations; features ready for acceptance with exact commands; decisions needed from the owner (brain move to agy, the parallel-dispatch proposal, anything blocked).
